# 03. OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Select RHEL AI, OpenShift AI, or a combined architecture for an enterprise workload
---
## Executive definition
RHEL AI provides a purpose-built, single-server AI foundation and inference-focused appliance experience, while OpenShift AI supplies a scalable, multi-user MLOps and GenAIOps platform on Kubernetes/OpenShift.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Choose the correct platform form factor.
- Explain coexistence patterns.
- Define scale and operational criteria.
- Build a defensible architecture decision record.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Single-Node Deployment
- Define what **single-node deployment** means for OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for single-node deployment.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for single-node deployment.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for single-node deployment, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for single-node deployment.
- Review how single-node deployment changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "rhoai-vs-rhel-ai"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: rhoai-vs-rhel-ai
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/rhoai-vs-rhel-ai
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Select RHEL AI, OpenShift AI, or a combined architecture for an enterprise workload
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for RHEL AI bootable image, with explicit consideration of single-node deployment.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for Red Hat AI Inference, with explicit consideration of cluster-scale operations.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI, using RHEL AI bootable image as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate single-node deployment, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate cluster-scale operations, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate inference, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate model customization, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate multi-tenancy, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate distributed compute, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate collaboration, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate hybrid-cloud portability, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The RHEL AI bootable image state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to cluster-scale operations.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 2: Validate identity and group mappings
**Goal:** Validate identity and group mappings for OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI, using Red Hat AI Inference as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for validate identity and group mappings; validate cluster-scale operations, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for validate identity and group mappings; validate inference, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI and is first observed around OpenShift AI.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass cluster-scale operations controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI and is first observed around model registry.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass inference controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 3: Workbench Remains Pending
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI and is first observed around Granite models.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Data distribution or quality shift.
- **First command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get storageclass`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass model customization controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 4: Pvc Does Not Bind
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI and is first observed around model serving.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Namespace or project mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get datasciencecluster -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc logs <pod> -n <namespace> --all-containers`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass multi-tenancy controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

- **First command:** `oc get dscinitialization -A -o yaml`
- **Second command:** `oc get resourcequota,limitrange -A`

- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass distributed compute controls.

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Corporate scenario drills
### Drill 1: Regulated Financial Service
- **Situation:** A regulated financial service must implement OpenShift AI vs RHEL AI with emphasis on single-node deployment.
- **Constraint:** Red Hat AI Inference has a strict availability, security, scale, or connectivity requirement.
- **Task:** Produce a design, implementation sequence, validation plan, rollback, and operating model.
- **Evidence expected:** Diagram, ADR, manifests, test output, SLO, runbook, risk register, and cost estimate.

- Start with business outcome, workload characteristics, data classification, and support constraints.

- State trade-offs, measurable acceptance criteria, evidence, ownership, and a rollback path.
- Avoid unsupported assumptions and confirm exact version, API stability, and operator dependencies.
